[Director 指令] [Patch — Round 18 升四路线] EMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL 5/25。Round 18 审稿 3.5→目标 4.0。以下 4 项内容修改，按优先级排序。引用不需要验证。

## Fix 1: SCB vs Huang et al. 新颖性论证（最高优先级）

**问题**: related_work.tex §2 "Distinction from CoT confirmation bias" 段落缺少与 Huang et al. (2024) 的 3 维度显式对比。当前 abstract 和 intro 用 "extending Huang's findings" 措辞，被 reviewer 认为是自我降级。

**修改要求**:
1. `related_work.tex` 第 13-14 行 "Distinction from CoT confirmation bias" 段落：补充一个 **3 维度显式对比**：
   - (a) 形式化：Huang 是 empirical observation；SCB 提供 Markov chain 形式化 (Observation 1, Remark 1)
   - (b) 诊断框架：Huang 没有可操作诊断工具；SCB 提供 3 个 zero-cost diagnostics (K_eff, BR, C_0)
   - (c) 排除范围：Huang 未测试 symbolic/formal verification；SCB 系统排除 27 种 training-free 变体
2. `abstract.tex` 第 1 行：把 "extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification" 改为更自信的表述，如 "providing independent formal evidence that extends beyond..."
3. `introduction.tex` 第 10 行：同样调整 "extends" 措辞为更自信表述
4. `discussion.tex` 第 9 行：同样调整

关键原则：**停止 "consistent with" / "extending" 自我降级**，改为明确声明 SCB 提供了 Huang 没有的东西。

## Fix 2: BR 重新框定为 degeneration 度量

**问题**: analysis.tex 中 BR 仍被称为 "bias ratio"，但 permutation null 已证明 BR_null ≈ BR_obs (p>0.7)，意味着 BR 完全由 base rate 解释，不是独立 bias 信号。

**修改要求**:
1. `analysis.tex` 第 9 行附近：permutation null 段落中添加 BR_excess = BR_obs - BR_null 的报告（数值：BR_excess ≈ 0，具体值从 "BR_null ≈ BR_obs" 推导）
2. 将 BR 框定调整：不改名（仍叫 bias ratio），但在描述中明确 BR 量化的是 "extraction degeneration degree"（约束退化到与 majority vote 等价的程度），而非独立的 bias 信号。当前第 9 行已有 "structural coupling rather than an independent bias signal" 表述，在此基础上强化为 degeneration framing。
3. 确保 `diagnosis.tex` 中 BR 相关描述与此一致

## Fix 3: Scope 边界明确化

**问题**: 论文在 SICA-specific 结论和 general 结论之间摇摆。

**修改要求**:
1. `discussion.tex`（当前仅 10 行）：扩展 scope 边界讨论，明确区分：
   - SICA-specific 结论：Observation 1 (source-attribution scoring 下的退化)、27 变体排除
   - General 结论：DPI bound (Remark 1) 适用于任何 deterministic self-extraction；SCB 跨 9 个模型普遍存在
   - Open questions：trained extractors 是否能打破 information ceiling；cross-architecture 方法的扩展性
2. 确保 abstract 和 conclusion 中的 claim scope 与 discussion 对齐

## Fix 4: Observation 1 补误差界 / 量化 degeneration

**问题**: Observation 1 ("Empirical support" 段落) 缺少量化精度。

**修改要求**:
1. `analysis.tex` 第 49-51 行 "Empirical support" 段落：补充具体数字说明 degeneration 程度（如 "Z3 rejects only 3.4% of constraints" → 量化 |C^∖| / |C| ≈ 0.034，confirming the degeneration condition |(C^∖)| ≈ 0"）
2. 补充 "over 90% of problems" 的具体数字（从 Tab:ablation 取：773/804 = 96.1% for Qwen, 类似数据）

所有修改后编译检查页数不超过 8 页正文。

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
5	\label{sec:pipeline}
6	\label{sec:condorcet}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), effective sample size reduces to
10	\begin{equation}
11	\label{eq:effk}
12	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
13	\end{equation}
14	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.
15	
16	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
19	
20	\looseness=-1
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:
24	\begin{equation}
25	\label{eq:maxsat}
26	\mathcal{C}^* = \arg\max_{\mathcal{S} \subseteq \mathcal{C}} \sum_{c \in \mathcal{S}} w_c \quad \text{s.t.} \quad \mathcal{S} \text{ is satisfiable.}
27	\end{equation}
28	Z3 \citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\texttt{answer\_a} == \texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.
29	
30	\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\mathcal{C}^{\setminus} = \mathcal{C} \setminus \mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:
31	\begin{equation}
32	\label{eq:score}
33	\text{score}(a) = \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^*} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a) - \alpha \sum_{c \in \mathcal{C}^{\setminus}} w_c \cdot \sigma(c, a),
34	\end{equation}
35	where $\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \arg\max_{a} \text{score}(a)$.
36	
37	\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:
38	\begin{equation}
39	\label{eq:degenerate}
40	\text{score}(a) \approx \sum_{\substack{c \in \mathcal{C}^* \\ \text{source}(c) \to a}} w_c,
41	\end{equation}
42	equivalent to majority voting. We show in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.
43	
44	\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\{q : |\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\}| / |\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\pm}0.49$\,pp across $\alpha \in [0, 1]$).
45	
46	\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \emph{bias ratio}:
47	\begin{equation}\label{eq:mirage}
48	\text{BR}(M,T) = \frac{P(c \text{ supports } a_M \mid a_M \neq a^*)}{P(c \text{ supports } a^* \mid a_M \neq a^*)},
49	\end{equation}
50	where $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies structural coupling between constraints and the majority answer under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1); values above 1 reflect the trace-count base rate and are expected whenever constraints are source-attributed. $\text{BR} = 1$ indicates no coupling beyond chance. We validate this metric empirically in \S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.
51	
52	\subsection{Experimental Setup}
53	\label{sec:exp-setup}
54	
55	\looseness=-1
56	\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}); Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces since closed-source APIs do not support self-extraction.
57	
58	\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} (exploratory, $n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\%$).
59	
60	\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\% of overhead.
61	
```

1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. No condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.112$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal.
6	
7	\begin{table*}[t]
8	\centering
9	\small
10	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). No condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$); $^{3s}$\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\%$ for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}.}
11	\label{tab:main_results}

14	Model & Domain & $n$ & SC (\%) & SICA (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\

17	Mistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\

43	\begin{figure}[t]
44	\centering
45	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}
46	\caption{SICA $\Delta$ (pp) vs.\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: nominally significant degradation (raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$).}
47	\label{fig:regime-scatter}
48	\end{figure}
49	
50	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. We trace the failure mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.
51	
52	\looseness=-1 TOST equivalence tests \citep{lakens2017equivalence} with pre-registered margin $\pm 2$\,pp---based on the smallest meaningful SC gain reported in \citet{wang2023sc}---confirm four conditions as equivalent; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}2$\,pp (MDE 1.86--4.84\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:power}). TOST sensitivity analysis across three margins ($\pm 1$, $\pm 2$, $\pm 3$\,pp) confirms that equivalence conclusions are margin-dependent (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise (mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\times$ FOLIO confirms the pattern ($\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp).
53	
54	\looseness=-1 Oracle controls replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}).
55

Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}\n\\caption{SICA $\\Delta$ (pp) vs.\\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \\geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: nominally significant degradation (raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$).}\n\\label{fig:regime-scatter}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. We trace the failure mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with pre-registered margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp---based on the smallest meaningful SC gain reported in \\citet{wang2023sc}---confirm four conditions as equivalent; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}2$\\,pp (MDE 1.86--4.84\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). TOST sensitivity analysis across three margins ($\\pm 1$, $\\pm 2$, $\\pm 3$\\,pp) confirms that equivalence conclusions are margin-dependent (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise (mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the pattern ($\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Oracle controls replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n",

    }

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017V39JeJZfqERkuYAyEAynm`</summary>

```text
110	SICA effectiveness depends sharply on the accuracy regime (Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter}).
111	
112	\paragraph{Intermediate-SC region.} Mistral-7B on FOLIO (SC $\sim$50--60\%) shows positive directional trends in 2 of 4 seeds: $\Delta = +3.43$\,pp ($p = 0.092$) and $+2.94$\,pp ($p = 0.070$); the remaining seeds show null or negative results. No seed reaches $p < 0.05$; the 4-seed mean is $+1.47$\,pp. The default-seed and seed-456 runs share identical SC baselines (55.88\%) yet diverge ($+3.43$ vs.\ $0.00$\,pp), suggesting that seed-level constraint quality, not SC level alone, influences the effect.
113	
114	\paragraph{Type A (SC $\geq$ 60\%).} All nine Type~A conditions produce no significant positive effects. Even in-context learning with gold logical examples yields $\Delta = +0.50$\,pp (n.s.). Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter shows significant degradation ($\Delta = -2.50$\,pp, $p = 0.011$).
115	
116	\paragraph{Type C (SC $\leq$ 40\%).} Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($n = 600$, SC $= 39.33\%$) shows $\Delta = -2.83$\,pp ($p = 0.014$): SICA \emph{significantly hurts} by formalizing incorrect reasoning. When $C_0 < 5\%$, nearly all traces agree and SICA reduces to SC by construction.
117	
118	Four supporting analyses confirm the mechanism: constraint ablation shows both MAX-SAT and count-only each agree with SC on ${>}$96\% of predictions ($n = 804$; Table~\ref{tab:ablation}); 91.8\% of constraints are discriminative but mirror the majority; effects concentrate in high-entropy problems ($+12.8$\,pp) with direction determined by accuracy regime; and hyperparameter peaks coincide with low SC baselines (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}).
119	
120	\begin{figure}[t]
121	\centering
122	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}
123	\caption{SICA $\Delta$ (pp) vs.\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: significant degradation ($p = 0.014$).}
124	\label{fig:regime-scatter}
125	\end{figure}
126	
127	
128	\subsection{Remediation: 25+ Strategies Fail}
129	\label{sec:remediation}
```

50	
51	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Two conditions are jointly necessary for any positive trend: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
52	
53	This correlation manifests through a specific mechanism: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.
54	
55	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.85\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88{\times}^{\dag}$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ ($^{\dag}$based on 4/12 traces due to extraction failures; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that 75.9\% of the potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.
56	
57	\begin{table}[t]
58	\centering
59	\small
60	\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\kappa$ descending). $\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\,$p < 0.05$; positive $\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$, 4 traces each.}
61	\label{tab:independence}
62	\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}
63	\toprule
64	Model & Condition & $\kappa$ & $K_{\text{eff}}$ & SC (\%) & $\Delta$ (pp) \\

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01589Wb3qLAv36W66HyQWqXv`</summary>

```text
130	\label{sec:interventions}
131	
132	We test more than twenty remediation strategies spanning prompt engineering, method-level redesign, and ten additional directions. All produce neutral or negative results.
133	
134	\paragraph{Prompt-level interventions.} Nine strategies spanning six dimensions (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:remediation}) all fail. Contrastive prompting reduces non-discriminative constraints from 8.2\% to 6.0\%, but newly discriminative constraints still mirror the majority ($\Delta = -1.1$\,pp). Cross-model extraction fails in both directions: weaker models yield 0\% discriminative constraints; stronger models produce accuracy within $\pm 1$\,pp of self-extraction. Multi-temperature sampling ($T \in \{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\}$) yields $\Delta = 0.00$\,pp.
135	
136	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\Delta = -9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\ref{app:remediation}). Ten further experiments (code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles) produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
137	
138	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, forming a same-model trade-off ceiling: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.85\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) suggests weaker models suffer more severe confirmation bias; all models exceed $1.0$, a pattern that persists from 7B open-source to frontier closed-source (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\%$ implies degeneration, SC $\geq 60\%$ implies confirmation bias, SC $\leq 40\%$ implies error amplification. Whether trained extractors shift the observed trade-off boundary remains open.
139	
140	\begin{table}[t]
141	\centering
142	\small
143	\caption{Independence-accuracy trade-off (FOLIO, $n = 204$). Top: aggregation methods on Mistral-7B; bottom: SC across three model families. All families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$, confirming an architecture-independent ceiling. $\kappa$: Fleiss' kappa on raw answers.}
144	\label{tab:kappa_generalization}
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; we provide independent evidence that extends \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We systematically diagnose symbolic confirmation bias as the mechanism, identify the structural degeneration within source-attribution scoring pipelines (Observation~1), and confirm it empirically across nine models and 27 training-free variants, localizing the failure to extraction rather than verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator     "oldString": "Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. Methodologically, SCB is formalized via a Markov chain structure (Observation~1, Remark~1) and confirmed through systematic exclusion of 27 training-free alternatives (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The key distinction: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful; SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted.",
    "newString": "Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \\citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}\nSelf-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \\citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}). \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \\citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\\kappa$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our findings extend this limitation to formal self-verification, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. Methodologically, SCB is formalized via a Markov chain structure (Observation~1, Remark~1) and confirmed through systematic exclusion of 27 training-free alternatives (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}). The key distinction: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful; SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \\citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \\citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \\citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Training-based verification.}\nThe methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \\citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Observation~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Remark~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.",

CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. The key distinction: CoT bias implies individual traces may be unfaithful; SCB shows that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting when self-extracted.",
          "+Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}.     "originalFile": "Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Method}\n\\label{sec:method}\n\n\\subsection{SICA Pipeline}\n\\label{sec:pipeline}\n\\label{sec:condorcet}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}), effective sample size reduces to\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:effk}\nK_{\\text{eff}} = K \\,/\\, [1 + (K{-}1) \\cdot \\kappa]\n\\end{equation}\n(following \\citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\\text{eff} \\approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.\n\nSymbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}).\n\n\\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\\{t_1, \\ldots, t_K\\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\\% using true quantifiers. The \\emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds the largest-weight satisfiable subset:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:maxsat}\n\\mathcal{C}^* = \\arg\\max_{\\mathcal{S} \\subseteq \\mathcal{C}} \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{S}} w_c \\quad \\text{s.t.} \\quad \\mathcal{S} \\text{ is satisfiable.}\n\\end{equation}\nZ3 \\citep{demoura2008z3} solves this with a 10-second timeout. Answer assertion constraints ($\\texttt{answer\\_a} == \\texttt{True}$, weight 1) ensure every candidate receives a nonzero base score, making SICA a strict generalization of SC.\n\n\\paragraph{Scoring.} Let $\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus} = \\mathcal{C} \\setminus \\mathcal{C}^*$. Each candidate $a$ is scored by:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:score}\n\\text{score}(a) = \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^*} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a) - \\alpha \\sum_{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}} w_c \\cdot \\sigma(c, a),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\sigma(c, a) = 1$ if $c$ was extracted from any trace producing answer $a$, and $\\alpha = 0.5$. The selected answer is $a^* = \\arg\\max_{a} \\text{score}(a)$.\n\n\\paragraph{Degeneration condition.} If self-extracted constraints are coupled to their source answer, MAX-SAT retains nearly all ($|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$), and scoring degenerates to:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:degenerate}\n\\text{score}(a) \\approx \\sum_{\\substack{c \\in \\mathcal{C}^* \\\\ \\text{source}(c) \\to a}} w_c,\n\\end{equation}\nequivalent to majority voting. We show in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias} that this degeneration occurs in practice.\n\n\\paragraph{The $C_0$ diagnostic.} The multi-candidate rate $C_0 = |\\{q : |\\mathcal{A}(q)| > 1\\}| / |\\mathcal{Q}|$ is a zero-cost diagnostic: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC. $\\alpha = 0.5$ is not critical (${\\pm}0.49$\\,pp across $\\alpha \\in [0, 1]$).\n\n\\paragraph{Bias ratio.} We quantify extraction bias via the \\emph{bias ratio}:\n\\begin{equation}\\label{eq:mirage}\n\\text{BR}(M,T) = \\frac{P(c \\text{ supports } a_M \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)}{P(c \\text{ supports } a^* \\mid a_M \\neq a^*)},\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $c$ is a self-extracted constraint, $a_M$ the model's majority answer, and $a^*$ the ground truth. BR quantifies structural coupling between constraints and the majority answer under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1); values above 1 reflect the trace-count base rate and are expected whenever constraints are source-attributed. $\\text{BR} = 1$ indicates no coupling beyond chance. We validate this metric empirically in \\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}.\n\n\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{sec:exp-setup}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). We also evaluate the universality of symbolic confirmation bias via cross-model extraction on five frontier models (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}); Mistral-7B extracts constraints from target traces since closed-source APIs do not support self-extraction.\n\n\\paragraph{Datasets.} FOLIO~\\citep{han2022folio} ($n{=}204$, with gold logical annotations), ProofWriter depth-5~\\citep{tafjord2021proofwriter} ($n{=}600$), LogiQA~\\citep{liu2020logiqa} ($n{=}200$). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} (exploratory, $n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).\n\n\\paragraph{Parameters.} $K{=}12$ traces at $T{=}0.7$, extraction at $T_{\\text{ext}}{=}0.3$, $\\alpha{=}0.5$, Z3 timeout 10s. McNemar exact test ($\\alpha{=}0.05$); 95\\% bootstrap CIs ($10{,}000$ resamples) for $n \\geq 200$. SICA incurs $4.5{\\times}$ SC wall-clock; extraction accounts for 76\\% of overhead.\n",

  "old_string": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. Three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). Observation~1 is specific to source-attribution scoring pipelines; the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies more broadly to any deterministic self-extraction. Empirical validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA paradigm.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, extending \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open.",
  "new_string": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. We distinguish two levels of generality. \\emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape this collapse. \\emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline, and symbolic confirmation bias (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary, and how cross-architecture verification scales to stronger generators, remain open."
}

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}). Observation~1 is specific to source-attribution scoring pipelines; the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies more broadly to any deterministic self-extraction. Empirical validation covers 27 training-free variants within the SICA paradigm.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, extending \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary remains open.
10	
```

Observation~1 is specific to source-attribution scoring pipelines; the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies more broadly to any deterministic self-extraction.   "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. We distinguish two levels of generality. \\emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape this collapse. \\emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline, and symbolic confirmation bias (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThis principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary, and how cross-architecture verification scales to stronger generators, remain open."
}

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. We distinguish two levels of generality. \emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape this collapse. \emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction pipeline, and symbolic confirmation bias (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale, suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) operationalize this principle and correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with decision boundaries generalizing prospectively to held-out model families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence and capability ordering (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors can shift this boundary, and how cross-architecture verification scales to stronger generators, remain open.
10	
```

          " \\looseness=-1",
          "-This principle has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01FkurjSvVT4GpVPiXjHUgL1`</summary>

```text
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$; Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our findings extend this limitation to formal self-verification, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.
11	
```

    "newString": "our formal analysis advances beyond this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}\nSelf-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \\citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}). \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \\citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\\kappa$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our findings extend this limitation to formal self-verification, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \\citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \\citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \\citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \\citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Training-based verification.}\nThe methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \\citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Observation~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Remark~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.",

Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our findings extend this limitation to formal self-verification, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.",
          "+We distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis advances beyond this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.",
          " ",
          " \\looseness=-1",
          " \\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}"
        ]
      }

## P0 修复（必须全部完成）

### 1. 引用修正（MF-1）

```bibtex
% 修正 byerly2026selfconsistency → byerly2025selfconsistency

  author={Byerly, Adam and Khashabi, Daniel},

  year={2025},
  note={arXiv:2411.01101}
}

% 修正 dupo2025
@article{dupo2025,
  title={{DuPO}: Enabling Reliable {LLM} Self-Verification via Dual Preference Optimization},
  author={She, Shuaijie and Bao, Yu and Lu, Yu and Xu, Lu and Li, Tao and Zhu, Wenhao and Huang, Shujian and Cheng, Shanbo and Lu, Lu and Wang, Yuxuan},
  journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2508.14460},
  year={2025}
}
```

注意 byerly 的 cite key 从 `byerly2026selfconsistency` 改为 `byerly2025selfconsistency`，related_work.tex L6 的 `\citep{..., byerly2026selfconsistency}` 需同步更新。

### 2. "advances beyond" → "extends and formalizes"（MF-2）

全文 5 处需同步修改：

1. **abstract.tex**: "advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification" → "extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings for formal self-verification"

2. **introduction.tex L10**: 整句改为：
"We **show** this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis **extends and formalizes** this empirical finding, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification."
（同时完成 SF-8 的 "prove" → "show"）

3. **related_work.tex L10**: "our formal analysis advances beyond this empirical finding" → "our formal analysis extends and formalizes this empirical finding"

4. **related_work.tex L14**: "SCB further advances beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding" → "SCB further extends and formalizes \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding"

5. **discussion.tex L9**: "advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification" → "extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings for formal self-verification"

### 3. Abstract 砍到 ≤198 词（SF-2 + MF-4 联动）

当前 ~202 词，超 EMNLP 200 词硬限。修改策略：
- 开头句 "We find no evidence of improvement" → "We find no evidence of improvement under self-extraction"（加 scope 限定，+3 词）
- 需要其他地方砍 ~7 词来补偿。候选：
  - "a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}" 可删（abstract 后文已用 SCB）→ 但这个在 abstract 里不存在
  - "chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT" → "chain-of-thought traces via weighted MAX-SAT"（砍 3 词）
  - 找其他冗余词
- 最终 abstract 必须 ≤198 词（留 2 词余量）

### 4. "pre-registered" → "pre-specified"（SF-3）

在 experiments.tex 中搜索 "pre-registered"，全部替换为 "pre-specified"。

### 5. Turpin et al. 年份修正（SF-6）

references.bib: `turpin2024language` 的 `year={2024}` → `year={2023}`。cite key 也从 `turpin2024language` 改为 `turpin2023language`，全文同步更新引用。

### 6. "We prove" → "We show"（SF-8）

introduction.tex L10 已在 MF-2 修复中一并处理。请确认没有其他地方使用 "prove" 描述 Observation 1。

## P1 修复

### 7. SCB framing 限定（SF-1）

在 analysis.tex §4.1 首次定义 SCB 处（约 L7），在 "symbolic confirmation bias" 后加限定：
"---a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines, not a cognitive bias analogy"

这与 related_work.tex L13-14 已有的 "structurally distinct from CoT-level confirmation bias" 形成呼应。

### 8. BH 区分 confirmatory vs exploratory（SF-4）

在 experiments.tex 中，BH correction 说明处区分：
- Confirmatory: 6 primary conditions (one model × one dataset × one seed)
- Exploratory: multi-seed aggregates and variant conditions
- BH 校正仅用于 confirmatory subset，exploratory 单独报告

### 9. 引用审计

修完以上所有后，对 references.bib 做一次全量审计（reference-audit skill）。重点检查：
- 所有 arXiv 引用的作者名、年份、标题是否与 arXiv ID 匹配
- 是否有其他幻觉引用
- 年份是否使用正式发表年份而非 arXiv 年份

## 约束
- 修改后 main text 必须 ≤8 页
- Abstract ≤198 词
- 不改论文核心 claim 和实验数据
- 不改 \enlargethispage 等排版 hack（SF-7 不修复）

50	\looseness=-1 Figure~\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\leq 40\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\geq 60\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. We trace the failure mechanism in \S\ref{sec:analysis}.

TOST sensitivity analysis across three margins ($\pm 1$, $\pm 2$, $\pm 3$\,pp) confirms that equivalence conclusions are margin-dependent (Appendix~\ref{app:tost}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise (mean $\Delta = +0.16 \pm 2.70$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\times$ FOLIO confirms the pattern ($\Delta \in [0.00, -2.01]$\,pp).
53	
54	\looseness=-1 Oracle controls replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\sim}$12\% of this ceiling (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}).

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QSUrTcH93w8Q8AhQb2jy8X`</summary>

```text
1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2

```text
1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp.
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis advances beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.
11	
12	\looseness=-1
13	Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\S\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
14	
15	Our contributions are:
16	\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
17	\item \textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We systematically diagnose symbolic confirmation bias as the mechanism, identify the structural degeneration within source-attribution scoring pipelines (Observation~1), and confirm it empirically across nine models and 27 training-free variants, localizing the failure to extraction rather than verification (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \S\ref{sec:remediation}).
18	
19	\item \textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
20	
21	\item \textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}).
22	\end{enumerate}
23	
24	\begin{figure}[t]
25	\centering
26	\small
27	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tikzpicture}[
28	    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,
29	    box/.style={draw, rounded corners=2pt, minimum height=0.55cm,

41	\node[box, right=of dedup] (maxsat) {MAX-SAT\\(Z3)};
42

44	\node[box, below=0.7cm of sample, fill=gray!12] (sc) {Majority\\vote};

      "content": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale.
