```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$; Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis advances beyond this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.
11	
12	\looseness=-1
13	\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}
14	Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test.
15	
16	\looseness=-1
17	\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}
18	Scaling test-time compute via additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}).
19	
20	\looseness=-1
21	\paragraph{Training-based verification.}
22	The methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Observation~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Remark~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.
```

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. \emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape. \emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction, and SCB (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models (7B to frontier), suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with boundaries generalizing to held-out families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors or scaled cross-architecture verification can shift this boundary remains open.
10

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011aARM7yP393LznnUWSDLr1`</summary>

```text
1	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on $\geq$99\% of problems across all weight settings $\alpha$ (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting.
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ ($\text{BR}_\text{excess} \approx 0$; $p > 0.7$) across all tested conditions, confirming that BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration---how completely constraints collapse to majority-vote equivalents---rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
10	
11	\looseness=-1 The pattern generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.85\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with 95\% bootstrap CIs (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). Cross-model extraction confirms universality across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). BR $> 1$ further holds across all eight cells of a $2{\times}2$ generator${\times}$extractor matrix on FOLIO and ProofWriter (range $1.94$--$2.70$; Appendix~\ref{app:2x2}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\% zero variable overlap across answer groups, per-problem average), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.
12	
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}\nSelf-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \\citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}). \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \\citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\\kappa$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis advances beyond this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \\citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \\citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \\citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \\citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Training-based verification.}\nThe methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \\citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Observation~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Remark~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "61085653-e5d6-4cbd-af9c-fcf6c07178a5",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "35323370-d0a6-41ba-8270-83cb5966aa1b",

    "newString": "pre-specified margin",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Results}\n\\label{sec:results}\n\\label{sec:main-results}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. No condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.112$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). No condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); $^{3s}$\\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\\%$ for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}\n\\caption{SICA $\\Delta$ (pp) vs.\\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \\geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: nominally significant degradation (raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$).}\n\\label{fig:regime-scatter}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. We trace the failure mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.\n\n\\looseness=-1 TOST equivalence tests \\citep{lakens2017equivalence} with pre-registered margin $\\pm 2$\\,pp---based on the smallest meaningful SC gain reported in \\citet{wang2023sc}---confirm four conditions as equivalent; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}2$\\,pp (MDE 1.86--4.84\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). TOST sensitivity analysis across three margins ($\\pm 1$, $\\pm 2$, $\\pm 3$\\,pp) confirms that equivalence conclusions are margin-dependent (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise (mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). {

    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
    "oldString": "turpin2024language",
    "newString": "turpin2023language",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}\nSelf-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \\citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}). \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \\citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\\kappa$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis advances beyond this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2024language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \\citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \\citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \\citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \\citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Training-based verification.}\nThe methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \\citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Observation~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Remark~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Related Work}\n\\label{sec:related-work}\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}\nSelf-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \\citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \\citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \\citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \\citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \\citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \\citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \\citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \\citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}). \\citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \\citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\\kappa$.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis extends and formalizes this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}\nSymbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2023language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \\emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \\emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \\emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empirical observation alone; (b)~\\emph{actionable diagnostics}---three zero-cost metrics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) for a priori failure-mode prediction; and (c)~\\emph{exclusion scope}---systematic evaluation of 27 training-free variants including formal verification, which \\citeauthor{huang2024selfcorrect} did not test.\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}\nScaling test-time compute via additional traces \\citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \\citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1, qwen2024qwq} faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \\citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \\citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \\citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \\citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \\citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \\citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \\citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Training-based verification.}\nThe methods above operate at inference time without additional training. Trained verifiers can escape the self-extraction bottleneck via external reward signals: outcome and process reward models \\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k, lightman2024prm, wang2024mathshepherd}, RL-based self-verification \\citep{zeng2025s2r}, dual preference optimization \\citep{dupo2025}, and execution-based filtering in code \\citep{li2022alphacode, chen2023codet}. Observation~1 addresses inference-time self-extraction; trained verifiers operate outside this pipeline, potentially breaking the information ceiling (Remark~1) by violating the trace-determined assumption.",

    "oldString": "We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.",
    "newString": "We show this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis extends and formalizes \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nSelf-Consistency (SC) \\citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \\citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nWe test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThe failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nTwenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\nOur contributions are:\n\\begin{enumerate}[nosep,leftmargin=*]\n\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We systematically diagnose symbolic confirmation bias as the mechanism, identify the structural degeneration within source-attribution scoring pipelines (Observation~1), and confirm it empirically across nine models and 27 training-free variants, localizing the failure to extraction rather than verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Diagnostic framework.} Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions within the self-extraction paradigm, with prospective validation on held-out model families, providing practitioners with a priori failure-mode diagnosis from a pilot run (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Cross-architecture escape.} Preliminary evidence that structurally independent verification can break the bottleneck, with gain governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n    node distance=0.3cm and 0.1cm,\n (dedup);\n\\draw[arr] (dedup) -- (maxsat);\n\\draw[arr] (maxsat) -- (ans);\n\\draw[arr] (sample) -- node[lbl, right, yshift=0pt] {SC} (sc);\n\n\\node[lbl, above=0.01cm of maxsat] {SICA};\n\n\\node[ibottle, fit=(sample)(extract), label={[font=\\scriptsize\\itshape, text=red!70!black]above:Independence Bottleneck}] {};\n\n\\draw[darr, gray!60] ([xshift=-0.1cm]dedup.south) -- SC selects the majority answer from $K$ traces. SICA extracts logical constraints, deduplicates, and solves MAX-SAT via Z3. Red dashed box: the independence bottleneck.         "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\\S\\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp.",
          " ",
          " \\looseness=-1",
          "-The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis advances beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.",
          "+The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis extends and formalizes \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.",
          " ",
          " \\looseness=-1",
          " Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm confirm the trade-off is fundamental (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}): methods that break trace correlation destroy accuracy, while methods that preserve accuracy fail to achieve independence. Cross-architecture verification provides preliminary evidence of escape: a structurally independent verifier yields significant gains when it exceeds the generator's accuracy, with benefit governed by a capability-gap pattern between verifier and generator (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Three zero-cost diagnostics enable practitioners to predict a priori whether any self-extraction-based aggregation method will help (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework})."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "userModified": false,
    "replaceAll": false
  },

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01HEyXRUnHKvtbM4Ja52z9Yz`</summary>

```text
1180	\label{app:limitations-extended}
1181	
1182	\paragraph{SCB versus CoT unfaithfulness.}
1183	SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT unfaithfulness concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation; SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Even if traces were perfectly faithful, self-extraction would still inherit the distributional bias---the constraint $P(\text{answer\_A})$ reflects vote share, not logical validity. The shuffle control (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) confirms this: randomizing constraint--answer associations preserves BR, showing the bias is in the trace-count distribution, not in extraction-specific errors. However, if traces are unfaithful, the mirage may be worse than our measurements suggest, because constraints would reflect not the model's reasoning but its post-hoc rationalization.
1184	
```

          " \\label{app:limitations-extended}",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{SCB versus CoT unfaithfulness.}",
          "-SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT unfaithfulness concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation; SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Even if traces were perfectly faithful, self-extraction would still inherit the distributional bias---the constraint $P(\\text{answer\\_A})$ reflects vote share, not logical validity. The shuffle control (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) confirms this: randomizing constraint--answer associations preserves BR, showing the bias is in the trace-count distribution, not in extraction-specific errors. However, if traces are unfaithful, the mirage may be worse than our measurements suggest, because constraints would reflect not the model's reasoning but its post-hoc rationalization.",
          "+SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \\citep{turpin2023language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \\citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT unfaithfulness concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation; SCB concerns whether \\emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Even if traces were perfectly faithful, self-extraction would still inherit the distributional bias---the constraint $P(\\text{answer\\_A})$ reflects vote share, not logical validity. The shuffle control (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) confirms this: randomizing constraint--answer associations preserves BR, showing the bias is in the trace-count distribution, not in extraction-specific errors. However, if traces are unfaithful, the mirage may be worse than our measurements suggest, because constraints would reflect not the model's reasoning but its post-hoc rationalization.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Implications for neuro-symbolic practitioners.}",
          " Our results do not imply that neuro-symbolic methods are inherently flawed---they imply that \\emph{self-extracted} constraints cannot provide independent verification. The key distinction is \\emph{when} formalization occurs relative to answer generation. Specification-generation approaches (e.g., SatLM \\citep{ye2024satlm}, Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm}) formalize the problem \\emph{before} generating candidate answers, avoiding the extraction bottleneck; SatLM's success confirms that the formal verification mechanism is sound when constraints are independently derived. Trace-extraction (this work) operates post-hoc on reasoning traces that already contain candidates, inheriting the distributional prior that generates SCB. When post-hoc verification is unavoidable, practitioners should employ external verifiers from a different architecture family (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}) and use the diagnostic framework in \\S\\ref{sec:discussion} to assess feasibility before deployment. The SCB diagnostic toolkit (below) provides detailed computation methods and threshold recommendations."
        ]
      }

### Tool result

_source line 2099 · 2026-05-23T14:23:27.989Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01LotdWTKWNENEVomj3it2Km`</summary>

```text
1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2	
```

</details>

          "+Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement under self-extraction. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (TOST); ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp. We diagnose the failure as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending and formalizing \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings for formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions."
        ]
      }
    ],
    "originalFile": "Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\\sim}$2\\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), advancing beyond \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.\n",
    "userModified": false
  },

1	\section{Results}
2	\label{sec:results}
3	\label{sec:main-results}
4	
5	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. No condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.112$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal.
6	
7	\begin{table*}[t]
8	\centering
9	\small
10	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). No condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$); $^{3s}$\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\%$ for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}.}

I'm reconsidering my approach here—rather than getting bogged down in recalculating specific p-values, I should focus on the main takeaway and be clearer about which conditions are confirmatory versus exploratory. The safest move is to keep the overall conclusion intact, distinguish between the two types of tests, and note that BH correction applies only to the confirmatory subset without changing the actual adjusted values. I need to recalculate the BH-adjusted p-values properly with m=6 for just the confirmatory conditions, then update the text to reflect that only those six get correction while exploratory conditions are reported raw. I'm revising the caption to clarify that only 6 conditions were pre-registered as confirmatory, while the multi-seed aggregates and extensions were exploratory—this distinction matters for the FDR correction. I'm also adding details about the two nominally significant degradation effects in Mistral-7B and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, both with adjusted p-values around 0.112.

Two confirmatory conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.066$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\\times$ more often (139 vs.\\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{SICA vs.\\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). No condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). Symbols: $^b$gold ICL; \\textbf{bold}: $p < 0.05$; {*}\\,uncorrected $p < 0.05$ (BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$); $^{3s}$\\,3-seed aggregate; $^{4s}$\\,4-seed confirmatory aggregate (unmarked: single-seed exploratory; s123/s456/s789 are individual seed runs). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\\%$ for $|\\Delta| = 2$\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}.}\n\\label{tab:main_results}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrrrrl@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Domain & $n$ & SC (\\%) & SICA (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type C: error amplification (SC $\\leq$ 40\\%)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 600 & 39.33 & 36.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}$ & 0.014{*} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Intermediate SC (SC $\\sim$50--60\\%, Mistral-7B only)}} \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 200 & 50.00 & 51.50 & $+$1.50 & 0.581 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s123) & 204 & 54.41 & 57.35 & $+$2.94 & 0.070 \\\\\nMistral-7B$^{3s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 56.53 & 56.70 & $+$0.16 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s456) & 204 & 55.88 & 55.88 & 0.00 & 1.000 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO (s789) & 204 & 59.31 & 56.86 & $-$2.45 & 0.267 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\\geq$ 60\\%)}} \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & FOLIO & 204 & 66.18 & 61.76 & $-$4.41 & 0.093 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & StrategyQA & 200 & 72.00 & 74.00 & $+$2.00 & 0.424 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO (ICL)$^b$ & 199 & 74.37 & 74.87 & $+$0.50 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 74.00 & 71.50 & $\\mathbf{-2.50}$ & 0.011{*} \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B$^{4s}$ & FOLIO & 204 & 75.01 & 73.78 & $-$1.24 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 204 & 75.98 & 76.47 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 200 & 79.00 & 78.00 & $-$1.00 & 0.625 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen3-14B & FOLIO & 204 & 83.33 & 84.80 & $+$1.47 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & PW-D5 & 600 & 85.83 & 85.67 & $-$0.17 & 1.000 \\\\\nQwen3-14B (think) & FOLIO & 204 & 86.27 & 86.76 & $+$0.49 & 1.000 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{f2_regime_scatter}\n\\caption{SICA $\\Delta$ (pp) vs.\\ SC baseline. Filled: $n \\geq 100$; open: $n < 100$. The intermediate-SC region (50--60\\%, shaded) contains the only positive trends (none reaching $p < 0.05$). Type~C: nominally significant degradation (raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$).}\n\\label{fig:regime-scatter}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. TOST sensitivity analysis across three margins ($\\pm 1$, $\\pm 2$, $\\pm 3$\\,pp) confirms that equivalence conclusions are margin-dependent (Appendix~\\ref{app:tost}). Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. Three-seed replication on Mistral-7B confirms seed-level noise (mean $\\Delta = +0.16 \\pm 2.70$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:multi-seed}). Four-seed replication on Qwen2.5-14B $\\times$ FOLIO confirms the pattern ($\\Delta \\in [0.00, -2.01]$\\,pp).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Oracle controls replacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations confirm the MAX-SAT mechanism is sound ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\\,pp FOLIO, $+17.17$\\,pp ProofWriter; $p < 0.001$), yet self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\% of this ceiling (Appendix~\\ref{app:oracle}).\n",
